03/12/2024, 6:25 pm - +44 7912 225796: ### **Complete List for CRM Dashboard Features**

Here’s a consolidated, detailed list of features and widgets for the CRM dashboard, organized by functionality to ensure clarity and developer readiness.

---

### **1. Client Overview and Categorization**
- **Client Categories (A, B, C, D)**:
  - Display a **pie chart or bar graph** showing the distribution of clients across categories:
    - A: High revenue, high priority.
    - B: Medium revenue, consistent compliance.
    - C: Low revenue, occasional interaction.
    - D: Inactive or low-priority clients.
  - Show total client count and active vs. inactive clients for each category.
- **Client Types**:
  - A **table or graph** showing client distribution by type:
    - Self-Employed: Focus on personal tax returns.
    - Limited Companies:
      - VAT Registered.
      - Non-VAT Registered.
    - Show percentage of each client type and total count.
  - Provide filters to view data specific to client types.

---

### **2. Service Packages**
- **Package Distribution**:
  - Display a pie chart showing the number of clients under:
    - Core Package: Bookkeeping, tax returns, accounts filing.
    - Compliance Package: VAT filings, payroll, regulatory submissions.
    - Growth Package: Strategic advisory, reporting, business growth services.
- **Package Metrics**:
  - Revenue generated by each package.
  - Overdue tasks and deadlines specific to each package.

---

### **3. Task Management**
- **Overdue Tasks**:
  - A **list of overdue tasks**, categorized by:
    - Client type (Self-Employed, Ltd, VAT/Non-VAT).
    - Client category (A, B, C, D).
    - Days overdue (e.g., 1-3 days, >7 days).
  - Include filters for task type (e.g., Tax Returns, VAT Filings).
- **Tasks Due Today**:
  - List tasks due for the current day with their statuses (Not Started, In Progress, Pending).
- **Upcoming Tasks**:
  - Display tasks due in the next 7-30 days with priority indicators.
- **Task Progress Summary**:
  - A bar chart showing:
    - Total tasks assigned.
    - Tasks completed.
    - Tasks pending.
    - Tasks overdue.
- **Task Completion Metrics**:
  - Show the percentage of tasks completed on time for the user.

---

### **4. Deadline Management**
- **Service-Specific Deadlines**:
  - **Calendar Widget**:
    - Display deadlines based on services:
      - Self-Employed: Tax returns and self-assessment deadlines.
      - Limited Companies: Annual accounts, VAT filings, confirmation statements.
      - VAT Registered Clients: Quarterly submission deadlines.
      - Non-VAT Registered Clients: Annual compliance tasks.
  - **Upcoming Deadlines**:
    - Show a list of imminent deadlines grouped by:
      - Service type.
      - Client type.
    - Overdue deadlines highlighted in red.
- **Deadline Progress**:
  - A progress bar showing the percentage of deadlines met vs. overdue for the current period.

---

### **5. Time Tracking and Productivity**
- **Time Tracker Widget**:
  - Real-time progress bar showing:
    - Hours logged today vs. daily target.
    - Weekly total logged hours vs. expected hours.
  - **Task Time Allocation**:
    - Table showing time spent on individual tasks grouped by:
      - Client.
      - Task type (e.g., accounts filing, payroll processing).
- **Unlogged Time Alerts**:
  - Notification for unlogged time on assigned tasks.
- **Utilization Rate**:
  - Display the ratio of logged time to total working hours (e.g., 85% productive).

---

### **6. Revenue and Client Insights**
- **Revenue by Client Category**:
  - Bar chart showing revenue generated by A, B, C, D category clients.
- **Revenue by Client Type**:
  - Revenue comparison for Self-Employed, Ltd (VAT/Non-VAT Registered).
- **Fee vs. Time Allocation**:
  - Compare client fees to time spent servicing them (efficiency metric).
- **Outstanding Fees**:
  - Table showing unpaid invoices by client with overdue amounts highlighted.
- **Top Clients by Revenue**:
  - Highlight the top 5 clients contributing the most revenue.

---

### **7. Client Reviews and Referrals**
- **Pending Reviews**:
  - List of clients who haven’t provided reviews yet.
  - Include follow-up reminders for pending reviews.
- **Recent Reviews**:
  - Display the most recent client feedback with ratings and comments.
- **Referral Status**:
  - Show referral statistics:
    - Clients asked for referrals.
    - Referrals received.
  - Include follow-up reminders for unacknowledged referral requests.

---

### **8. Notifications and Alerts**
- **Task Alerts**:
  - Notifications for:
    - Overdue tasks.
    - Tasks nearing deadlines.
- **Client Status Alerts**:
  - Notifications for:
    - New clients onboarded.
    - Clients who left with logged reasons.
    - Clients with no activity in the past 30 days.
- **Review and Referral Reminders**:
  - Alerts for pending reviews or referrals.
- **Daily Summary Alerts**:
  - At the start of the day, provide a summary of:
    - Tasks due today.
    - Overdue tasks.
    - Total hours to log.

---

### **9. Visual Reports and Graphs**
- **Task Status Overview**:
  - Pie chart showing task distribution:
    - Completed, In Progress, Pending, Overdue.
- **Client Overview**:
  - Bar chart or table showing:
    - Distribution by category (A, B, C, D).
    - Distribution by type (Self-Employed, Ltd, VAT/Non-VAT Registered).
- **Service Package Summary**:
  - Pie chart showing the number of clients in Core, Compliance, and Growth packages.
- **Time Logged vs. Target**:
  - Progress bar or graph showing logged hours vs. target hours for the day/week.

---

### **10. Advanced Features**
- **Customizable Dashboard**:
  - Allow users to select and rearrange widgets based on their preferences.
- **Leaderboard (Optional)**:
  - Introduce a leaderboard to gamify performance:
    - Rank team members by tasks completed, hours logged, or overdue tasks resolved.
- **Historical Trends**:
  - Line graph showing:
    - Weekly average hours logged.
    - Monthly task completion rates.

---

### **Example Dashboard Layout**

| **Widget**                  | **Description**                                                                 |
|-----------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Client Overview**         | Distribution of clients by category (A, B, C, D) and type.                      |
| **Service Packages**        | Pie chart showing clients by Core, Compliance, and Growth packages.             |
| **Tasks by Status**         | Pie chart of task distribution (Completed, Pending, Overdue).                   |
| **Overdue Tasks**           | List of overdue tasks categorized by priority and client type.                  |
| **Upcoming Deadlines**      | Calendar displaying deadlines for services like VAT, tax returns, accounts.     |
| **Time Tracker**            | Display of daily/weekly time logged vs. target hours.                           |
| **Revenue Summary**         | Bar chart showing revenue by client category and type.                          |
| **Reviews and Referrals**   | Pending reviews and referral statistics with follow-up reminders.               |
| **Weekly Performance**      | Tasks completed, time logged, and overdue task resolution rate.                 |

---

This comprehensive list includes all the necessary features for managing and visualizing team performance, client insights, and deadlines effectively. Let me know if there are any specific additions or adjustments needed!

06/03/2025, 10:22 pm - +44 7912 225796: ### **AML Risk Assessment Algorithm**

#### **Step 1: Assign Weightings to Risk Indicators**
Each question is categorized into **High, Medium, or Low** risk factors, with corresponding scores.

| Question | Risk Weighting | Score |
|----------|--------------|-------|
| Is the client's business primarily cash-based? | High | 3 |
| Does the client intend to use the firm's bank for transactions? | High | 3 |
| Is there a lack of evidence of trade? | High | 3 |
| Is it an overseas client? | High | 3 |
| Is there a complex group structure? | High | 3 |
| Has there been any change in business activity? | Medium | 2 |
| Is there any intra-group trading? | Medium | 2 |
| Has there been a dramatic increase in the turnover? | Medium | 2 |
| Does the client have high turnover from small business locations? | Medium | 2 |
| Does the client require a client account denominated in a foreign currency? | Medium | 2 |
| Were there any transactions in the year without an immediate or obvious purpose? | High | 3 |
| Have we met the client? (If **NO**) | High | 3 |
| Is the client's risk level high? | High | 3 |
| Does the client have a history of persistent and unlikely error in the tax returns? | High | 3 |
| Does the client have any proof of address? (If **NO**) | High | 3 |
| Is the client's ID verified? (If **NO**) | High | 3 |

---

#### **Step 2: Calculate Total Score**
- **Each "YES" answer contributes its assigned score.**
- **Each "NO" answer contributes 0, unless specified otherwise.**

Formula:
\[
\text{Total Risk Score} = \sum (\text{Risk Scores of answered YES})
\]

---

#### **Step 3: Risk Categorization Based on Score**
- **Low Risk**: Score **0 - 6**  
- **Moderate Risk**: Score **7 - 14**  
- **High Risk**: Score **15 and above**  

---

### **Implementation Logic for the Developer**
1. **Create a form or questionnaire** for the user to answer each of the 16 questions.
2. **Assign a score** for each "YES" answer.
3. **Sum the scores** from all answered questions.
4. **Determine risk level** based on the final score.
5. **Output the risk level** as **Low, Moderate, or High** risk.

---

### **Example Cases**
| Client Responses | Score | Risk Level |
|-----------------|------|------------|
| "No" to all questions | 0 | Low Risk |
| "Yes" to 3 medium-risk questions (e.g., turnover increase, intra-group trading, overseas client) | 6 | Low Risk |
| "Yes" to 4 medium-risk questions and 2 high-risk questions | 14 | Moderate Risk |
| "Yes" to multiple high-risk questions (cash-based, no ID verification, unusual transactions, etc.) | 18 | High Risk |

---

24/03/2025, 9:16 pm - +44 7912 225796: Core Goals
	1.	Track time accurately while user works across multiple applications
	2.	Associate the time directly with a specific task
	3.	Pause/resume automatically or manually to ensure accurate data
	4.	Prevent abuse or forgetfulness with reminders or idle detection

⸻

Recommended Timer Workflow

Step 1: Timer UI on Task Page

Include:
	•	Start, Pause, Resume, and Stop buttons
	•	A visible count-up timer (HH:MM:SS)
	•	Real-time status (e.g. “Timer running”, “Paused”, “Completed”)
	•	Optional: “Add manual time” (if someone forgets to start timer)

⸻

Step 2: Backend Logic

When user clicks “Start”
	•	Save timestamp (start_time) in the database
	•	Start a timer that runs in the background, even if user switches tabs/apps
	•	Change task status to “In Progress”

When user clicks “Pause” or leaves idle for X mins
	•	Save a pause_time
	•	Calculate time elapsed = pause_time - start_time
	•	Add to a total_time_logged field
	•	Pause backend timer

When user clicks “Resume”
	•	Save new start_time
	•	Restart the backend timer

When user clicks “Stop/Complete Task”
	•	Save final stop_time
	•	Add elapsed time to total_time_logged
	•	Finalise entry
	•	Change task status to “Completed”

⸻

Step 3: Idle Detection (Important in Your Case)

Since users work across multiple apps, implement:

Browser-side detection (if CRM is open)
	•	If no keyboard/mouse activity for, say, 5 minutes, show a prompt:
“You’ve been inactive for 5 minutes. Do you want to pause the timer?”

Fallback (if user switches tabs)
	•	Use visibilitychange JavaScript event:

document.addEventListener("visibilitychange", function() {
  if (document.hidden) {
    // Option: Pause timer or start idle timer
  }
});



Optional: Keep Alive Ping

Send heartbeat pings every minute while the timer is active. If no ping is received for 5–10 mins, mark the time as idle or alert user.

⸻

Step 4: Auto-Save

Ensure timer values are auto-saved every X seconds or after each action (start, pause, resume). Use browser local storage as a backup to avoid data loss on refresh.

⸻

Step 5: Display Time Logs

On the task detail or history tab:
	•	Show time sessions (e.g. “2:15 PM – 3:05 PM”, “Paused at 3:10 PM”)
	•	Total time logged: e.g. 1 hr 42 mins
	•	Option for admin to edit/correct logs

⸻

Optional Enhancements
	•	Tag software used (via browser extension)
	•	Time estimate vs actual comparison for performance reports
	•	Auto-complete task when timer stops

⸻

09/05/2025, 8:29 pm - +44 7912 225796: Step 1: Tenant Adds Their SMTP Settings in Remindoo
Tenants fill a form in your app:

Field	Example
SMTP Server	smtp.office365.com / smtp.zoho.com / smtp.gmail.com
SMTP Email	info@accotax.co.uk
SMTP Username	Same as SMTP Email
SMTP Password	(App password or real password — stored encrypted)
SMTP Port	587
SMTP Security	TLS
From Name	ACCOTAX

Save these in smtp_settings table in Supabase, scoped to each tenant_id.

🔧 Step 2: Send Emails via Backend Function (Nodemailer)
Use Supabase Edge Function or external Node.js backend to send email dynamically based on tenant's SMTP.

js
Copy
Edit
const nodemailer = require("nodemailer");

async function sendTenantEmail({ to, subject, html, tenantId }) {
  const smtp = await getSmtpConfigFromSupabase(tenantId);

  const transporter = nodemailer.createTransport({
    host: smtp.server,
    port: smtp.port,
    secure: false,
    auth: {
      user: smtp.username,
      pass: decrypt(smtp.password)
    },
    tls: { rejectUnauthorized: false }
  });

  await transporter.sendMail({
    from: `"${smtp.fromName}" <${smtp.email}>`,
    to,
    subject,
    html

  return { success: true };
}

09/05/2025, 8:30 pm - +44 7912 225796: ## ✅ SCALABLE APPROACH FOR TENANT EMAIL SENDING

### 🟢 **Model: Use Tenant’s Own SMTP (Direct Integration)**

Instead of using AWS SES for tenants, **let tenants enter their own SMTP credentials** (e.g. Gmail, Outlook, Zoho, Office365, etc.). You just act as a relay.

This means:

* No domain verification needed
* No AWS SES complexity
* Works with any email provider tenants already use

---

## ✅ STEP-BY-STEP IMPLEMENTATION

---

### 🔧 Step 1: Tenant Adds Their SMTP Settings in Remindoo

Tenants fill a form in your app:

| Field         | Example                                                   |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| SMTP Server   | `smtp.office365.com` / `smtp.zoho.com` / `smtp.gmail.com` |
| SMTP Email    | `info@accotax.co.uk`                                      |
| SMTP Username | Same as SMTP Email                                        |
| SMTP Password | (App password or real password — stored encrypted)        |
| SMTP Port     | 587                                                       |
| SMTP Security | TLS                                                       |
| From Name     | ACCOTAX                                                   |

Save these in `smtp_settings` table in **Supabase**, scoped to each `tenant_id`.

---

### 🔧 Step 2: Send Emails via Backend Function (Nodemailer)

Use **Supabase Edge Function** or **external Node.js backend** to send email dynamically based on tenant's SMTP.

```js
const nodemailer = require("nodemailer");

async function sendTenantEmail({ to, subject, html, tenantId }) {
  const smtp = await getSmtpConfigFromSupabase(tenantId);

  const transporter = nodemailer.createTransport({
    host: smtp.server,
    port: smtp.port,
    secure: false,
    auth: {
      user: smtp.username,
      pass: decrypt(smtp.password)
    },
    tls: { rejectUnauthorized: false }
  });

  await transporter.sendMail({
    from: `"${smtp.fromName}" <${smtp.email}>`,
    to,
    subject,
    html
  });

  return { success: true };
}
```

🔐 Password should be stored securely (AES encryption or Supabase Vault).

---

### 🔧 Step 3: Trigger This Backend via Bubble

* Use **Bubble API Connector** to call your function.
* Pass:

  * `tenant_id`
  * `to`, `subject`, `body` (html/text)

---

09/05/2025, 8:31 pm - +44 7912 225796: Here’s a complete implementation pack to support **per-tenant SMTP email sending** using their own SMTP settings in your **Remindoo** app built on **Bubble + Supabase**.

---

## ✅ 1. Supabase Table Schema (for SMTP Settings)

Create the following table in Supabase:

```sql
CREATE TABLE tenant_smtp_settings (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT uuid_generate_v4(),
  tenant_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenants(id),
  smtp_server text NOT NULL,
  smtp_email text NOT NULL,
  from_name text,
  smtp_username text NOT NULL,
  smtp_password_encrypted text NOT NULL, -- encrypted before saving
  smtp_port integer DEFAULT 587,
  smtp_security text DEFAULT 'TLS', -- options: TLS, SSL, None
  created_at timestamp DEFAULT now()
);
```

---

## ✅ 2. SMTP Encryption Helper (Node.js Backend Example)

```js
const crypto = require('crypto');

const SECRET_KEY = process.env.SMTP_ENCRYPTION_KEY; // 32 bytes
const IV = Buffer.alloc(16, 0); // Static IV (or generate securely per record)

function encrypt(text) {
  const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv('aes-256-cbc', Buffer.from(SECRET_KEY), IV);
  let encrypted = cipher.update(text);
  encrypted = Buffer.concat([encrypted, cipher.final()]);
  return encrypted.toString('hex');
}

function decrypt(text) {
  const encryptedText = Buffer.from(text, 'hex');
  const decipher = crypto.createDecipheriv('aes-256-cbc', Buffer.from(SECRET_KEY), IV);
  let decrypted = decipher.update(encryptedText);
  decrypted = Buffer.concat([decrypted, decipher.final()]);
  return decrypted.toString();
}
```

---

## ✅ 3. Supabase Edge Function: Send Email with Tenant’s SMTP

Use [Supabase Edge Functions](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/functions) or your own backend:

```js
const nodemailer = require("nodemailer");
const { createClient } = require('@supabase/supabase-js');
const { decrypt } = require('./crypto'); // from above

const supabase = createClient(process.env.SUPABASE_URL, process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY);

exports.handler = async (event) => {
  const { tenant_id, to, subject, html } = JSON.parse(event.body);

  const { data: smtp } = await supabase
    .from('tenant_smtp_settings')
    .select('*')
    .eq('tenant_id', tenant_id)
    .single();

  const transporter = nodemailer.createTransport({
    host: smtp.smtp_server,
    port: smtp.smtp_port,
    secure: false,
    auth: {
      user: smtp.smtp_username,
      pass: decrypt(smtp.smtp_password_encrypted)
    },
    tls: {
      rejectUnauthorized: false
    }
  });

  await transporter.sendMail({
    from: `"${smtp.from_name}" <${smtp.smtp_email}>`,
    to,
    subject,
    html
  });

  return {
    statusCode: 200,
    body: JSON.stringify({ success: true })
  };
};
```

## ✅ 4. Bubble API Connector Setup

### ➤ **Step-by-Step Setup**

Go to **Plugins > API Connector > Add API**:

| Field          | Value                               |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| Name           | `TenantSMTP`                        |
| Authentication | None (or bearer if using edge auth) |

### ➤ **Add API Call: `Send Email`**

* **Use as:** Action
* **Method:** POST
* **URL:** `https://your-backend-url/send-tenant-email`
* **Headers:**

  * `Content-Type`: `application/json`
* **Body type:** JSON (raw)

**Body JSON:**

```json
{
  "tenant_id": "<tenant_id>",
  "to": "<email_to>",
  "subject": "<email_subject>",
  "html": "<email_html>"
}
```

Replace with dynamic values from Bubble.

Hello,

[AWS Health may periodically trigger reminder notifications about this communication if resources remain unresolved.]

We are contacting you as we have identified that your AWS Account currently has one or more AWS Lambda functions using the Node.js 18 runtime.

We are ending support for Node.js 18 in Lambda on September 1, 2025. This follows Node.js 18 End-Of-Life (EOL) reached on April 30, 2025 [1]. End of support does not impact function execution. Your functions will continue to run. However, they will be running on an unsupported runtime which is no longer maintained or patched by the AWS Lambda team.

As described in the Lambda runtime support policy [2], end of support for language runtimes in Lambda happens in several stages.

- Starting on September 1, 2025, Lambda will no longer apply security patches and other updates to the Node.js 18 runtime used by Lambda functions, and functions using Node.js 18 will no longer be eligible for technical support. Also, Node.js 18 will no longer be available in the AWS Console, although you can still create and update functions using Node.js 18 via AWS CloudFormation, the AWS CLI, AWS Serverless Application Model (SAM), or other tools.

- Starting October 1, 2025, you will no longer be able to create new Lambda functions using the Node.js 18 runtime.

- Starting November 1, 2025, you will no longer be able to update existing functions using the Node.js 18 runtime.

We recommend that you upgrade your existing Node.js 18 functions to the latest available Node.js runtime in Lambda before September 1, 2025.

Your impacted Lambda functions using the Node.js 18 runtime are listed on the 'Affected resources' tab of your AWS Health Dashboard.

This notification is generated for functions using the Node.js 18 runtime for the $LATEST function version. The following command shows how to use the AWS CLI [3] to list all functions in a specific region that is using Node.js 18, including published function versions. To find all such functions in your account, repeat the following command for each region:

aws lambda list-functions --region us-east-1 --output text --query "Functions[?Runtime=='nodejs18.x'].FunctionArn"

Starting 180 days before deprecation, you can also use Trusted Advisor to identify all functions using the Node.js 18 runtime [4].

If you have any concerns or require further assistance, please contact AWS Support [5].

04/06/2025, 4:41 pm - +44 7912 225796: Reference (RASV-8401-WWTN)


Dear Waqas

Thank you for your query.  Unlike the MTD services (where JSON-based submissions are sent to the endpoint documented in the HMRC Developer Hub) the other services that you have listed involve the submission of XML through the Transaction Engine.  Accordingly, the technical specifications for these services are documented in a different place on GOV.UK.

Please refer to our main page for XML-based services, at Software development for HMRC: detailed information - GOV.UK.  There, you'll find links to the relevant services, including:

Corporation Tax online
PAYE online
Real Time Information
Self-Assessment online (non-MTD)
and also associated or supporting information, such as

Basic guide for XML software developers
Transaction Engine
Agent Authorisation online - this links to the specifications you'd need to implement agent authorisation for the above XML-based services.
Please read through those specifications which relate to what you are intending to develop.  If you have any questions not covered in those specs, or need clarification, please email this team at sdsteam@hmrc.gov.uk.  In your subject line, please mention 'XML' and identify which service(s) your query relates to - this will help us route your query as needed - and we'll do what we can to help.

We value your opinion and would love to hear about your recent experience with our team. Click here to provide your feedback and help us improve our service.

Kind regards, 

Sean McCreesh
Software Developer Support Team | CDIO | HM Revenue & Customs | HMRC Developer Hub: https://developer.service.hmrc.gov.uk/api-documentation
To help us improve our service please complete a short survey Give us feedback.
You can join the HMRC user panel and participate in future user research sessions by Signing up here. 
If you’re unsure whether an email is from HMRC:
Do not reply to it or click on any links
Forward it to phishing@hmrc.gov.uk
Keeping your data secure

Sending information over the internet is generally not completely secure, and we can’t guarantee the security of your data while it’s in transit. Any data you send is at your own risk.

We have procedures and security features in place to keep your data secure once we receive it.

13/06/2025, 9:06 pm - Suhaib Osmani: 📌 Remindoo v0.02 Patch Notes

NEW FEATURES

✅ Role creation – Users can now create custom roles to better align with their organization’s structure.

✅ Permissions for each module and main feature – Granular permissions have been implemented for precise access control.

✅ Visibility of clients based on permissions – Clients are only visible to users with appropriate viewing rights.

✅ Visibility of tasks based on permissions – Users can now see only the tasks they’re permitted to access.

✅ Compatibility of permissions with assignee and other filters – Permissions now work seamlessly with filters like assignees for secure, accurate data views.

✅ Assignees filter in client table – Filter clients by assigned users for better workload management.

✅ Assignees filter in task table – Tasks can now be filtered by assignees for clearer delegation.

✅ Services can now be defined as Company House Services – Added a toggle to mark services for Companies House tracking.

✅ Service templates sync Company House Service status – Updating a service template will now update all services made from it.

✅ Frontend for related clients – Related clients are now visible in the client table and during client onboarding.

✅ Assignees table optimized in backend – Backend performance improved for handling assignee data more efficiently.


BUG FIXES

🐛 Fixed URL bypass for roles and permissions – Users can no longer access restricted areas via direct URLs.

🐛 Fixed permission deletion issue – Removing one permission no longer deletes all permissions for the role.

🐛 Fixed invisible assignees in client table – Assignee data now correctly displays in the client list.

🐛 Fixed client table search/filter compatibility – Filters and search now work correctly together in the client table.

🐛 Fixed task table search/filter compatibility – Task table search and filters now behave as expected.

🐛 Fixed AML 'No' answers bug – AML forms with all "No" answers are now correctly marked as completed.

🐛 Fixed missing confirmation statement tags – Tags now appear even when company status detail is empty.

🐛 Fixed column visibility search in client table – Search within column visibility settings now works properly.

🐛 Fixed onboarding data leak – Data from previously onboarded clients no longer appears in new onboardings.

🐛 Fixed personal tax onboarding failure – Contact-to-client onboarding for Personal Tax Return now works in all scenarios.

🐛 Fixed timer redirect dependency – Timer redirect works without needing to open the task table first.

🐛 Fixed incorrect timer log format – Start date, end date, and duration now display in the correct format.

🐛 Fixed incomplete task search – Task search bar now returns consistent, accurate results.

🐛 Fixed subtask step saving issues – Subtask steps now save correctly and show relevant errors when needed.

🐛 Fixed timer popup collapse animation – Timer popup now collapses smoothly with no animation glitches.

🐛 Fixed client table alignment issues – UI alignment problems in the client table have been resolved.

🐛 Fixed missing related clients in column visibility – "Related Clients" now appears correctly in the visibility settings popup.

🐛 Fixed non-functional timer checkbox in column visibility – Timer column visibility can now be toggled properly.

09/07/2025, 1:43 pm - +44 7912 225796: Your Scenario Recap
🏢 Company:
Year-end: March 2025

Added to system: 9th July 2025

Last year-end (2024) was already filed.

You prepare & file 2025 accounts → Companies House updates:

last_accounts_end_on → March 2025

next_accounts_end_on → March 2026

System sees March 2026 and creates tasks immediately ❌

🚫 The Problem
You're creating tasks too early (in July 2025) for an accounting year that ends in March 2026, which:

Doesn't even exist yet from a statutory perspective.

Should not trigger tasks until that year has ended (after 31st March 2026).

✅ The Required Fix
Modify your logic to only create tasks when:

js
Copy
Edit
next_accounts_end_on <= today
i.e. only if the year has ended or is today, not in the future.

🔧 Where to Add This Logic
In updateChosenServices()
Add the check before creating tasks via refresh_ch_tasks:

🔁 Updated Condition (when updateTasks = false)
Replace:

js
Copy
Edit
if (!updateTasks) {
With this:

js
Copy
Edit
if (!updateTasks) {
  const referenceDate = changedFields.find(({ field }) => field === refField)?.newValue;

  const today = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);

  // Only proceed if referenceDate is today or earlier
  if (referenceDate?.slice(0, 10) > today) {
    console.log("Future year end detected — skipping task creation for", referenceDate);
    return;
  }

  await supabase
    .from("chosen_services")
    .update({ custom_fields: updatedFields })
    .eq("id", service.id);

  const { data } = await supabase
    .schema("for_tasks")
    .rpc("refresh_ch_tasks", {
      p_reference_date: referenceDate,
      p_client_id: clientId,
      p_chosen_service: service.id
    });

  newTaskNames.push(data);
  return;
}

To protect your email deliverability, we have temporarily disabled DKIM signing for emails originating from remindoo.co. If you use Amazon Pinpoint to send mail through this domain, those emails will also be affected.

If the removal of the DNS records was intentional, you do not need to take any additional action. In 5 days, remindoo.co will no longer be considered to be configured for the purpose of DKIM signing.

If it was unintentional, please restore the DNS records to your DNS settings within 5 days. Once we confirm the presence of the DNS records, we will re-enable DKIM signing.

Please note that the settings for individually verified email addresses override domain-level settings. For example, if you enable DKIM signing for a verified domain but disable DKIM signing for a verified email address in that domain, then emails from that email address will not be DKIM-signed.

Please note that this email only relates to the US East (N. Virginia) region.

19/09/2025, 3:03 pm - +44 7912 225796: Thanks for the clarification — we’ll update the workflow to reflect the **new proposal flow**, where the user begins by selecting a **template**, entering **client details (with CH sync)**, defining **signatories**, and then proceeds to configure the **pricing section**.

Below is the **updated end-to-end workflow**, along with supporting detail.

---

# 🔄 **Updated Proposal Pricing Module – Workflow & Functional Specification**

*For: Product Manager, Developers & Designers*

---

## 🧾 Overview

This module enables users to create and send **bespoke accountancy/taxation proposals**. Pricing is based on:

* Services selected
* Turnover/volume bands
* Multipliers
* Optional extras

**Proposal creation starts with a template**, followed by **client sync, signatories**, and then custom pricing.

---

## ✅ A. Proposal Creation Workflow (Frontend)

### 📌 Step-by-Step

| Step   | User Action                                   | System Action                            | Notes                                       |
| ------ | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
| **1**  | **Select Proposal Template**                  | Loads predefined service set & structure | From saved template list                    |
| **2**  | **Name Proposal**                             | Creates a unique draft                   | e.g. “Q4 Proposal for XYZ Ltd”              |
| **3**  | **Enter Client Details**                      | Sync with Companies House                | Search by company name/number               |
| **4**  | **Select or Add Signatories**                 | Define who will sign the document        | Allow >1 signer, e.g. Director + Co-Founder |
| **5**  | **Review Preloaded Services (from template)** | Load services into pricing section       | Editable                                    |
| **6**  | **Select Business Type**                      | e.g. Ltd Company, Sole Trader            | Filters service applicability               |
| **7**  | **Enter Turnover**                            | System picks correct turnover band       | From admin-defined ranges                   |
| **8**  | **Enter Volume Values**                       | e.g. number of directors, transactions   | For volume-based fees                       |
| **9**  | **Select Multiplier Options**                 | e.g. Record quality, meeting type        | Stacked logic supported                     |
| **10** | **Pricing Auto-Calculated**                   | Show service fee breakdown & total       | Based on pricing engine                     |
| **11** | **Review Proposal Summary**                   | Editable line items + notes              | Manual override if needed                   |
| **12** | **Preview Final Proposal**                    | Document styled and branded              | Optional hide/show pence or multipliers     |
| **13** | **Send for eSignature**                       | Capture all signatories                  | Docusign/AdobeSign/HelloSign integration    |
| **14** | **Download / Email Copy to Client**           | Save in CRM, PDF, or send via email      | Optional archive                            |

---

## 🧾 B. Admin Settings (Backend)

### 📁 1. Templates

| Feature                   | Description                                          |
| ------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| Create Proposal Templates | e.g. “Standard Ltd Co Setup”, “Sole Trader VAT Only” |
| Preload Services          | Services, notes, layout                              |
| Assign Business Type      | Templates tied to specific business types            |
| Save & Clone              | Reusable structure for speed                         |

---

### 📊 2. Services & Pricing Types

Each service can use one of these pricing models:

| Pricing Type         | Description                            |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| Fixed Fee            | Same fee for all clients               |
| Fee by Turnover      | Multiplied by turnover band            |
| Fee by Volume        | Linked to range (e.g. number of staff) |
| Fee with Multipliers | Adjusted by additional options         |

#### Linked Data

* **Turnover bands**: Custom ranges (£0–£30k, etc.)
* **Volume bands**: Number-based ranges (e.g. 1–2 directors)
* **Multipliers**: Layered logic (see section D)

---

### 🔀 3. Multipliers (Details)

| Multiplier Name    | Options                                 | Example                      |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Quality of Records | Excellent (1.0), Shoebox (1.6), etc.    | Applied to Annual Accounts   |
| Account Ownership  | We manage (0.80)                        | Affects Bookkeeping          |
| Complexity Level   | Standard (1.0), Complex (2.5)           | Applies to Tax Return        |
| Meeting Location   | Zoom (1.0), Client Office (1.1)         | Used in VFD/Quarterly Review |
| Person Involved    | Partner (1.3), Account Manager (1.0)    | Applies to meeting pricing   |
| Payroll Timing     | 5 days before (1.00), 24h before (1.15) | Adjusts payroll fee          |

✅ Multipliers can be:

* **Single-select**
* **Multi-select**
* **Linked to turnover/volume**

---

## ⚙️ C. Pricing Calculation Logic

> **Final Fee per Service** =

```
(Base Fee OR Band Fee) 
× Turnover Multiplier (if applicable) 
× Volume Multiplier (if applicable) 
× Multiplier A × Multiplier B … (stacked)
```

### Example Calculation:

> **Annual Accounts Fee**

* Base: £85
* Turnover: £125k → 1.20
* Record Quality: Shoebox → 1.6
* Partners: 2–3 → 1.2

**Calculation:**
`£85 × 1.20 × 1.6 × 1.2 = £195.84`

---

## 📄 D. Proposal Document Output

Includes:

* Client name, company reg info (CH data)
* Services & pricing breakdown
* Fee logic (optional display of multipliers)
* Signatories
* Final total
* Company logo and contact info
* Download as PDF / Send for signature

---

## 🔐 E. System Rules & Validations

| Rule               | Logic                                                    |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------- |
| Minimum fee        | System enforces minimum quote total                      |
| Duplicate services | Prevent adding same service twice                        |
| Required fields    | Business type, turnover, volume, signatory               |
| Calculation        | Fees update live based on inputs                         |
| Multipliers        | All required multipliers must be selected before preview |

---

## 📌 F. Feature Flags / Optional Toggles

* Hide/show pence
* Show monthly vs annual equivalent
* Hide internal multiplier names from client
* Show notes under each service
* Lock pricing once sent for signature

---

## 🗃️ G. System Entities

| Entity        | Key Fields                         |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------- |
| Template      | Name, business type, services      |
| Proposal      | Client info, pricing, signatories  |
| Service       | Name, type, fee model, multipliers |
| Multiplier    | Label, options, values             |
| Turnover Band | From, To, Label                    |
| Volume Band   | From, To, Label                    |
| Signatory     | Name, Email, Role                  |

---

## 📈 H. Future Enhancements (Optional)

* CRM sync for proposal follow-ups
* Auto-flag expired proposals
* Clone previous proposals
* Version history / audit trail

---

## 🧑‍💻 For Dev Team – API & UX Notes

| Component            | API Requirement                         |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| Companies House Sync | CH API lookup by name/CRN               |
| Pricing Engine       | Calculation endpoint                    |
| PDF Export           | Proposal → PDF renderer                 |
| eSignature           | Docusign or HelloSign integration       |
| Multipliers          | Configurable via API for UI flexibility |

---

01/10/2025, 11:50 pm - Kaim Ahmad: @⁨~ACCOTAX - Chartered Accountants & Tax Consultants⁩ , 

Sir, I have pinpointed the exact issue as to why we're getting the December dates in our emails. 

- If you create a service with 0 tasks (as the trigger date is in the future), the system will wait for that date to create that task.
- Once that date is reached, the system has to create the task then push the dates forward by 1 interval (monthly/yearly/etc)
- This works fine if you already had a previous task. As this signifies "we have finished this period's work, so lets create the new task for the next period and update the dates"
- However, the case where a user starts off with 0 tasks was not properly being accounted for, as the dates are already set for the newly created task, but still the system was pushing those dates ahead by 1 period.
- This is why we were getting december dates for september tasks.

The fix for this is not huge, so I'm creating a fix for this and will push this asap. 

As I have pinpointed all the tasks/services that have issues in them, I will also fix their dates (with your permission).

Thanks.
01/10/2025, 11:52 pm - Kaim Ahmad: To summarize, we will have accounted for:

- Tasks being created on wrong dates
- Emails being sent with future dates

Dear Waqas

Thank you for your query.  Unlike the MTD services (where JSON-based submissions are sent to the endpoint documented in the HMRC Developer Hub) the other services that you have listed involve the submission of XML through the Transaction Engine.  Accordingly, the technical specifications for these services are documented in a different place on GOV.UK.

Please refer to our main page for XML-based services, at Software development for HMRC: detailed information - GOV.UK.  There, you'll find links to the relevant services, including:

Corporation Tax online
PAYE online
Real Time Information
Self-Assessment online (non-MTD)
and also associated or supporting information, such as

Basic guide for XML software developers
Transaction Engine
Agent Authorisation online - this links to the specifications you'd need to implement agent authorisation for the above XML-based services.
Please read through those specifications which relate to what you are intending to develop.  If you have any questions not covered in those specs, or need clarification, please email this team at sdsteam@hmrc.gov.uk.  In your subject line, please mention 'XML' and identify which service(s) your query relates to - this will help us route your query as needed - and we'll do what we can to help.

We value your opinion and would love to hear about your recent experience with our team. Click here to provide your feedback and help us improve our service.

Kind regards, 

Sean McCreesh
Software Developer Support Team | CDIO | HM Revenue & Customs | HMRC Developer Hub: https://developer.service.hmrc.gov.uk/api-documentation
To help us improve our service please complete a short survey Give us feedback.
You can join the HMRC user panel and participate in future user research sessions by Signing up here. 
If you’re unsure whether an email is from HMRC:
Do not reply to it or click on any links
Forward it to phishing@hmrc.gov.uk
Keeping your data secure

Sending information over the internet is generally not completely secure, and we can’t guarantee the security of your data while it’s in transit. Any data you send is at your own risk.

We have procedures and security features in place to keep your data secure once we receive it.

do not reply to it or click any links
forward it to phishing@hmrc.gov.uk

05/12/2025, 4:19 pm - +44 7912 225796: @⁨PM Adnan⁩ The Agent Authorisation Service allows an agent (e.g. an accountant) to be authorised to act on behalf of a client with HMRC (for services like PAYE, VAT, Corporation Tax, etc.).

🔧 Developer Checklist for Agent Authorisation (XML)
1. Register as a Software Developer with HMRC

Apply via HMRC's Software Developer Support Team (SDST).

You’ll receive:

Vendor ID

Access to test credentials

Permission to use HMRC's test environments

2. Obtain the Technical Pack for Agent Authorisation

Download the Agent Authorisation XML Technical Pack from HMRC. This includes:

XML schemas (XSDs) for request/response

Sample message formats

Business validation rules

Interface specifications

IRmark generation method (digital hash for message integrity)

3. Build the XML Submission Logic

Your software must:

Construct an Agent Authorisation Request XML message, including all required fields (agent ID, client details, service type, etc.)

Include a correctly calculated IRmark

Follow all schema rules and business logic (as defined in the tech pack)

4. Authenticate Using the Government Gateway

Your submission must:

Be routed through HMRC’s Transaction Engine

Use Government Gateway credentials (test or live)

Support authentication tokens and session handling

5. Test the Integration

Use HMRC’s test environments to validate your XML messages:

a. TPVS (Third Party Validation Service)

For structural and basic validation of your XML.

b. ETS (End-to-End Testing Service)

Simulates real submissions to HMRC’s systems.

Confirms that the authorisation is accepted and status responses are correctly handled.

Note: HMRC may require successful test scenarios before approving your product.

6. Apply for HMRC Recognition (Optional)

If you're developing commercial software (used by others), apply for HMRC software recognition for the Agent Authorisation service.

This is optional if you're only using the integration internally.

7. Go Live

Once tested and (optionally) approved:

Switch to live credentials

Use the live endpoint for submissions

Handle live responses and agent authorisation statuses

## Overview

Users are experiencing inconsistent behavior when attempting to save changes to templates and dates. Sometimes changes are saved automatically, while other times they are not, leading to frustration and repeated efforts to make the same changes.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open the template editor.
2. Change the template and date settings.
3. Attempt to save the changes.
4. Refresh the page.
5. Observe whether the changes have been saved or not.

## Acceptance Criteria

- The save functionality should consistently prompt the user to save changes before navigating away from the page.
- All changes made to templates and dates should be saved automatically or with a clear confirmation prompt.
- Users should not have to refresh the page to see if their changes have been saved.

26/02/2026, 5:14 pm - +44 7912 225796: ## Standard Operating Procedure for Setting Up New Proposals

### Objective

This SOP outlines the steps to create and manage new proposals effectively, ensuring all necessary information is captured and processed correctly.

### Key Steps

 

**1. Setting Up New Proposals** [0:00](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=0)

![generated-image-at-00:00:00](https://loom.com/i/ed4ee5b419474c1880897337da3d12d2?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Begin by selecting the option to set up new proposals.
- Ensure that existing needs are correctly identified and added.

 

**2. Adding Contact Information** [0:15](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=15)

![generated-image-at-00:00:15](https://loom.com/i/8eaba4c476da4a4d88a16119996ff2ea?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Check for a plus sign to add additional email addresses.
- Ensure the default area code is set to UK (+44) instead of Guernsey.

 

**3. Adding Additional Numbers** [0:40](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=40)

![generated-image-at-00:00:40](https://loom.com/i/5266270d18cc4d8393dc836a53b8dff1?workflows_screenshot=true)

- If the plus sign is missing, note that it should be there for adding multiple contacts.

 

**4. Entering Product Information** [1:04](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=64)

![generated-image-at-00:01:04](https://loom.com/i/b8eed30ee1c94aa6a898ddec603f7a05?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Add products to the proposal and ensure prices are correctly imported.
- Verify that the turnover range and corresponding charges are accurate.

 

**5. Applying Discounts** [2:12](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=132)

![generated-image-at-00:02:12](https://loom.com/i/f52c8ade87894998a903eb70da9d557b?workflows_screenshot=true)

- When adding discounts, ensure they apply immediately upon entry.
- Test various discount percentages to confirm functionality.

 

**6. Document Preparation Notification** [3:12](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=192)

![generated-image-at-00:03:12](https://loom.com/i/8eef740b60f840e29dabfd7c0fed650b?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Display a message indicating that documents are being prepared to manage client expectations.

 

**7. Viewing Attachments** [4:00](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=240)

![generated-image-at-00:04:00](https://loom.com/i/c5ef17e715054054a4c72c6956c41083?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Implement a 'view' button for any attached files instead of a download option.

 

**8. Generating Proposal Links** [5:06](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=306)

![generated-image-at-00:05:06](https://loom.com/i/5c3db86192494b3aac893c9a4ba9e740?workflows_screenshot=true)

- After sending the proposal, generate a link for clients to view the proposal.

 

**9. Email Confirmation** [5:20](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=320)

![generated-image-at-00:05:20](https://loom.com/i/437dbb15d2694848b93d177737ab3301?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Ensure that emails are sent correctly and check BCC settings if emails are not received.

 

**10. Standardizing Email Formats** [8:00](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=480)

![generated-image-at-00:08:00](https://loom.com/i/118e5db05d614346873ba54bcc8372c4?workflows_screenshot=true)

- All outgoing emails should include the Remindoo logo and follow a standard format.

 

**11. Troubleshooting Email Issues** [8:45](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=525)

- Investigate any issues with emails not being sent or received, especially for signed documents.

 

**12. Front-End Fee Display** [9:15](https://loom.com/share/e01549b7455e4b59aff1a96045981650?t=555)

- Ensure that fees added in the product section are displayed correctly on the front end.

### Cautionary Notes

- Always double-check that all information is accurate before sending proposals.
- Be aware of potential delays in email delivery and communicate this to clients if necessary.

### Tips for Efficiency

- Use templates for proposals to save time on formatting.
- Regularly review and update product pricing to ensure accuracy.
- Keep a checklist of common issues to troubleshoot quickly.

          <GovTalkDetails>

                    <Keys/>

                    <GovTalkErrors>

                              <Error>

                                        <RaisedBy>Department</RaisedBy>

                                        <Number>3001</Number>

                                        <Type>business</Type>

                                        <Text>The submission of this document has failed due to departmental specific business logic in the Body tag.</Text>

                              </Error>

                    </GovTalkErrors>

          <Body>

                    <ErrorResponse xmlns="http://www.govtalk.gov.uk/CM/errorresponse" SchemaVersion="2.0">

                              <Application>

                                        <MessageCount>1</MessageCount>

                              </Application>

                              <Error>

                                        <Type>Business Rule</Type>

                                        <Location/>

                              </Error>

                    </ErrorResponse>

          </Body>

13/03/2026, 5:22 am - +44 7912 225796: If you want to give selected users 6 months free without using any voucher or promotion code, the cleanest approach in Stripe is to apply the coupon directly to the customer’s subscription from the dashboard or through your system logic. This avoids the need for the user to enter any code.

Below is the practical method.

⸻

1. Create the Discount Coupon (One Time Setup)

In your Stripe dashboard:
	1.	Go to Billing → Coupons
	2.	Click Create Coupon

Set the following:
	•	Discount type: Percentage
	•	Amount: 100%
	•	Duration: Repeating
	•	Duration: 4 months

You choose 4 months because your system already has 60 days (≈2 months) trial, and you want 6 months total free access.

⸻

2. Automatically Apply It to Selected Users

When creating the subscription for special users, simply attach the coupon.

From Stripe Dashboard
	1.	Go to Customers
	2.	Open the customer profile
	3.	Click Create / Edit Subscription
	4.	Under Discount, select the coupon you created

Stripe will automatically apply it.

⸻

If You Apply It From Your App (API Logic)

When creating the subscription, include the coupon parameter.

Example logic:

Create subscription
Add coupon: 6monthsfree

Stripe will then:
	•	keep the 60-day trial
	•	apply 100% discount for the next 4 months

⸻

3. Resulting Billing Timeline

Example subscription price: £49/month

Period	Charge
Day 0–60	Trial → £0
Month 3	£0
Month 4	£0
Month 5	£0
Month 6	£0
Month 7 onward	£49/month

So the user receives 6 months total free access automatically.

⸻

4. Best Way to Use This

This method is ideal for:
	•	early adopters
	•	beta testers
	•	strategic partners
	•	internal users
	•	investors

because it requires no code and no special checkout flow.

⸻

5. Optional Control

When creating the coupon you can also:
	•	limit maximum redemptions
	•	set an expiry date
	•	restrict to specific pricing plans

This prevents misuse.

18/03/2026, 5:34 pm - +44 7912 225796: @⁨Sami AI Backend⁩ Adsum response : 1. Data Retrieval from HMRC

MTD VAT API Integration

Whenever a business has connected its HMRC account to our system, OpenTax retrieves VAT information directly via the VAT APIs provided by HMRC. This is an integration that ensures data is transferred via HMRC’s own secure channels.

Agent Services Account Retrieval

In situations where the MTD VAT API connection is not set up, OpenTax will retrieve the necessary VAT and tax information from the ASA and OSA that you, as the accountant, connect to our platform. We have developed specialised data-fetching methods to capture only the relevant information. This process is fully permission-based, requiring your explicit authorisation to ensure it remains secure and compliant.

Continuously Updated Integrations

We actively monitor HMRC’s API roadmap and update our integrations as new endpoints and data points become available. Our objective is to ensure data flows through the most direct and secure integration routes wherever possible.

2. How We Keep Your Data Secure

Encryption in transit and at rest

All data is encrypted in transit using industry-standard TLS and encrypted at rest within Amazon Web Services. We use AWS Key Management Service to manage encryption keys securely.

Access controls and least privilege

We apply strict role-based access controls using AWS Identity and Access Management. Access to sensitive data is restricted to authorised services and personnel on a least-privilege basis. Access is logged and monitored.

Security-focused infrastructure

Our infrastructure is designed around recognised security and data protection principles, including data minimisation, controlled access, and continuous risk review.

Any further general information is available

16/04/2026, 1:38 pm - Sami AI Backend: Aoa Sir,

After a detailed investigation of the issue, I discussed the root cause and possible solution with Umair Bhai.

We found that the issue is not with the syncing process itself, but rather due to the absence of a fallback mechanism during syncing. In some cases, the Gmail server does not provide the required metadata/details for certain email threads at the time of sync. Because of this, those threads are skipped and not stored in our system.

To address this, the proposed solution is to implement a fallback mechanism. Whenever such a case occurs, we will log the thread in the database as a failed entry. Then, a scheduled fallback process (e.g., running every hour) will retry fetching the missing data from Gmail and store it in the database once it becomes available.

The estimated time to implement this fallback mechanism is around 2 to 2.5 days. However, as per Umair Bhai’s suggestion, since this issue occurs very rarely and we currently have higher-priority tasks in the pipeline, we can plan to implement this fix at a later stage.

Please let me know if you’d like us to prioritize this or proceed as suggested.

Regards
16/04/2026, 6:52 pm - Suhaib Osmani: @⁨~ACCOTAX - Chartered Accountants & Tax Consultants⁩ @⁨Umair Saeed⁩ Sir, regarding the loe email issue, there is an issue with the confirmation email we send after the client has signed the proposal. We send the signed proposal document as an attachment, which, in some cases, becomes too large and causes the email to fail.

We have 2 options for the fix:

We remove the attachment altogether, and include a link that will allow the client to view and download the document.

OR

We place a check that allows us to send attachments unless the file is too large. Only in those cases, the system will remove the attachment and place a link in the email.

We can implement the fix after you confirm whether the attachment is necessary.

03/05/2026, 9:58 pm - +44 7912 225796: ## Overview

Several client portal and workflow features appear to be implemented incorrectly or are not behaving as expected. Users cannot clearly understand or complete actions such as inviting a client, submitting authorization requests, or adding proposals, and some saved data is not persisting after refresh. This creates confusion and blocks core workflows for client onboarding, authorization, and lead/proposal management.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open the client portal and navigate to the client invitation/contact area.
2. Add a client email/contact and click **Save**.
3. Return to the invitation area and observe the available actions such as **Draft client user**, **Save and submit**, **Edit**, and **Delete**.
4. Navigate to **Client Authorization** and submit a client authorization request.
5. Observe the loading/status text shown during submission and check whether the request status is visible on the card.
6. Edit client details, save them, then refresh or revisit the page to verify whether the data persists.
7. Open a lead profile and look for the expected **Add Proposal** / **View Proposal** button.
8. Check the knowledge base / AML-related areas to confirm whether recent updates are visible.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Client invitation flow should match the requested UX: a single clear button/action for inviting contacts, with no confusing or unrelated draft-user controls.
- Saving a contact/client email should persist correctly and remain available after refresh.
- Client authorization submission should display clear, user-friendly progress text and a visible status indicator on the card once submitted.
- Any action buttons that change state or package should show confirmation and/or a clear result of the action.
- Client details entered by the user must persist reliably and not disappear after a moment or page reload.
- Lead profile should include the expected proposal actions (e.g., **Add Proposal** / **View Proposal**) where specified.
- Knowledge base and AML-related updates should be visible and reflect the latest changes.

**1. Check article visibility filters in Knowledge Base** [0:00](https://loom.com/share/96f77c9e451845958768fe88db4abce7?t=0)

![generated-image-at-00:00:00](https://loom.com/i/92bb451ab49b4d9e8e6259ee74219d78?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Open **Knowledge Base**.
- Create or edit an article and set its visibility/tag to **Internal** or **External**.
- Verify the selected tag appears in the list/filter area.
- Confirm the visibility filters include **Internal**, **External**, and **Both**.
- Switch the filter to **Internal** and refresh the page.
- Expected: only internal articles remain visible; the selected filter/tag is shown correctly.

 

**2. Verify dashboard/account details display** [2:09](https://loom.com/share/96f77c9e451845958768fe88db4abce7?t=129)

![generated-image-at-00:02:09](https://loom.com/i/06df1ba69ac84e249bdcc26ff6e0700b?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Open the **Dashboard** and view an account record.
- Check that the following fields are displayed where applicable: 
  - **Year and date**
  - **Pay by reference number**
  - **VAT number**
  - **Full address**
  - **Enrolled more on SM-45PQ** (as referenced in the transcript)
- Open an **individual** account such as **ACAS**.
- Confirm company-only fields are hidden when not applicable: 
  - **Company number**
  - **Business UTR**
  - **VAT number**
  - **ERN**
- Confirm personal fields are shown instead, such as **personal tax number** and the relevant **SATR date**.

 

**3. Reproduce the eSign completion error** [3:13](https://loom.com/share/96f77c9e451845958768fe88db4abce7?t=193)

![generated-image-at-00:03:13](https://loom.com/i/b2fa166f2e144d04bce12b97b2629dc4?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Open **eSign**.
- Add a document to sign.
- Click **Finish** after completing the signing flow.
- Observe the result message.
- Expected behavior: the document should complete successfully.
- Actual behavior reported: 
  - Message shows **document submitted successfully**.
  - Then an **error occurred** message appears.
  - The document still appears as **unsigned** in the signing area, even though it is listed under **recently signed documents** and can be downloaded.

 

**4. Test client document sharing for task-linked uploads** [3:59](https://loom.com/share/96f77c9e451845958768fe88db4abce7?t=239)

![generated-image-at-00:03:59](https://loom.com/i/b8d3501ac633448ea7b3d8fb4135e147?workflows_screenshot=true)

- Open the **client document sharing / QIIME** flow.
- Send the email/invitation to the client.
- Have the client upload documents through the shared portal.
- Verify the system sends a notification to the tenant that the client uploaded documents.
- Check the related task: 
  - The uploaded documents should be linked to the task.
  - The task should contain a link to the folder where the client uploaded files are stored.
- Confirm the files are saved under the correct client folder and the correct return folder (for example, the transcript mentions a **tax return** folder).

*Unsubmitted* - Your client authorisation request has not been submitted. This may be because you have added more than the maximum number of clients allowed for one submission.

 *Failed* - The client authorisation request has failed HMRC's initial verification process. This may be because:-

- it is a duplicate request
- one or more of the client details supplied do not match the details HMRC hold
- the address supplied is one from which HMRC have previously had post returned

24/06/2026, 2:54 pm - Suhaib Osmani: Sir, in addition to the HMRC email, I wanted to confirm the functionality for the E-Sign module,

Firstly, if a user uploads a docx file, we will allow the user to do so, but convert that file to a pdf when using it for E-Sign.

Secondly, all of the recipients will be able to view all of the documents that were uploaded together. The user will not be able to select specific documents to hide from specific users when uploading multiple documents. (This is assuming that access control is not a required feature)

Thirdly, if a client is sent documents to sign without using the client portal, then a client portal user cannot be included in the same list of recipients.

Lastly, if multiple clients are sent multiple documents, one user will be required to fill all inputs in all of the documents, and this will update the status for the entire recipient list.

08/07/2026, 2:48 am - +44 7912 225796: @⁨maqddus Butool⁩ ![generated-image-at-00:00:09](https://loom.com/i/5a9583abd36b42d08520d54829c8db97?workflows_screenshot=true)

## Overview

Several settings and admin screens show inconsistent UI states and behaviors around saving, editing, and bulk changes. In multiple places the UI prompts to "save changes" even when the content is already saved, and the saved/editing color states appear reversed or inconsistent. Bulk actions such as changing role permissions, archiving users/clients, and sending invites to inactive/archived users also do not consistently warn the user or prevent invalid actions, which can lead to accidental destructive changes and confusion.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open any settings area where a value can be edited and saved (for example header/footer, email, view settings, or similar).
2. Click into an already-saved field and observe the status text and color state.
3. Create a new role, copy permissions from admin, then change one permission.
4. Open the employee list and attempt to send an invite or change status for an inactive/archived user.
5. Open screens such as teams, email template, variables, products, or package and compare save/close button behavior and modal layout.
6. Try adding or editing items where required fields are missing or where bulk changes affect multiple entries.
7. Observe whether the UI shows consistent prompts, prevents invalid actions, and clearly indicates saved vs editing states.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Saved items should display a clear "Saved" state, and editable items should display a clear "Editing" state; the colors/icons should be consistent across all settings screens.
- If an action changes multiple records or permissions at once, the UI must show a confirmation prompt explaining the scope of the change before applying it.
- Inactive or archived employees/clients should not be eligible for actions like sending invites or editing status unless explicitly allowed.
- Bulk status changes such as marking inactive/archived should warn about associated tasks and require confirmation before proceeding.
- Save/Close/Preview button labels and modal layouts should be consistent across all related dialogs.
- Missing required fields should be highlighted with a clear validation message indicating what is missing.
- Checkbox sizes, button styles, and other repeated controls should be standardized across the application.
- Any action that affects all permissions/items should provide an explicit "apply to all" control or confirmation, rather than changing everything silently.

## Overview

Multiple issues were observed across Leads, Proposals, and the proposal template editor, including inconsistent styling, missing/incorrect action buttons, navigation state loss, and failures when saving or sending proposals. These defects significantly disrupt the workflow because users cannot reliably edit templates, preserve changes, send emails, or trust the calculated proposal totals.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open **Leads** and review the lead sections/statuses.
2. Navigate to **Lead Settings** and save changes to lead statuses.
3. Open a lead and inspect the action buttons, proposal/timeline controls, and task actions.
4. Go to **Contacts &gt; Import from Companies** and import a contact record.
5. Open **Create Proposal** and proceed through the proposal flow.
6. In the proposal editor/template editor, add header/footer content, change font settings, and navigate away/back.
7. Refresh the page or return to the previous screen without explicitly saving.
8. Create or edit a proposal, add products, apply a discount, and preview the proposal.
9. Attempt to send the proposal email from the proposal flow or recent proposal preview.

**Observed issues:**

- Buttons are inconsistent in color/style (some should be blue but are not).
- Some screens show incorrect labels or misplaced actions (e.g., email/snooze, save placement).
- Changes in the template editor are lost when navigating back or refreshing.
- Proposal editor does not allow normal multiline text entry/bullets in some fields.
- Discount and total calculations appear incorrect in preview.
- Email sending fails in the proposal flow.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Lead, proposal, and template editor screens use consistent styling, spacing, and font sizing across the app.
- Primary action buttons are visually consistent (e.g., blue styling where required) and placed in the expected locations.
- Saving a template or proposal persists changes reliably, including after navigation or refresh.
- Returning to a previously edited screen does not discard unsaved state unexpectedly, or the user is clearly warned before losing changes.
- Proposal text fields support normal multiline input and bullet/paragraph formatting where applicable.
- Proposal discount and total calculations are accurate in both edit and preview states.
- Sending proposal emails works successfully from the proposal flow and recent proposal preview.
- Imported contacts are routed to the correct detail/edit flow so users can complete missing information immediately.

## Overview

Users are unable to reliably save customized column/field ordering in the lead and ask views. In some cases the selected order resets after saving or refreshing, and in other cases the order appears to apply in one area but not another, indicating inconsistent behavior across screens. Additionally, the timer field appears to be missing from the available hide/show options, so users cannot hide it even when they do not want it displayed.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open the lead view configuration screen.
2. Select fields such as Lead Name, Status, Created By, Pricing, Sent By, Trend Date, Creation Date, and Proposal Name in a custom order.
3. Click **Save**.
4. Refresh the page and observe that the configuration reverts to the default order instead of persisting.
5. Open the Ask view configuration screen.
6. Reorder fields such as Assignee, Client First Name, Client Last Name, and Services.
7. Save the changes and verify whether the selected order is preserved consistently.
8. Check the available fields for visibility toggles and attempt to hide **Timer**.
9. Observe that Timer is not available to hide in the configuration list.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Custom field/column ordering must persist after clicking **Save** and after page refresh.
- The saved order should be applied consistently across all relevant views/screens.
- Reordering fields in the Ask view should respect the exact selected order.
- All displayable fields, including **Timer**, should be available in the hide/show configuration if they are intended to be configurable.
- Changes should be validated with regression testing across all related areas to ensure behavior is consistent.

## Overview

Several screens in the client management flow have inconsistent UI, missing controls, and incorrect or stale data rendering. Users are unable to reliably track statuses such as AML, letter of engagement/clearance, and client visibility settings, and some actions (e.g. send/resend, close, search, save/proceed) are missing or behave inconsistently. These issues create confusion, make it difficult to complete workflows, and can cause users to work with outdated or incorrect client information.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open the client workflow and add or edit a client.
2. Review the client details, AML, risk assessment, document, and task-related sections.
3. Observe that several controls are missing or inconsistent (e.g. close buttons, search buttons, save/proceed, send/resend, all-selection controls).
4. Trigger AML or document-related actions and note that statuses are not always shown or updated immediately.
5. Change client visibility / column settings, save, refresh, and return to the page.
6. Check Manage Tasks and client lists to confirm whether filtering, assignee display, and selected column spans persist correctly.
7. Compare the UI styling across sections and note inconsistent fonts, colors, spacing, and button styles.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Client, AML, document, and task screens use consistent styling, spacing, font sizes, and button appearance.
- Missing controls are added where required, including close buttons, search buttons, save/proceed actions, resend/send actions, and all-selection controls.
- AML, letter of engagement, letter of clearance, and other workflow statuses are displayed correctly and update immediately without requiring a manual refresh.
- Client visibility / column selection settings persist after save and refresh.
- Manage Tasks filters only tasks relevant to the selected client.
- Assignees/service manager fields are visible and sortable where applicable, including a clear empty state when no assignee exists.
- Document and client actions do not show stale or incorrect values (for example, missing AML should not display as low risk).

* When an Engagement Letter (LOE) is signed, automatically email the signed copy (with attachments) to both the signer and the sender, and generate an in-app notification.
* Generate email notifications and in-app notifications whenever a client performs key actions, including:

  * Signing documents
  * Uploading documents
  * Adding comments
* Ensure fully signed documents are automatically sent to both the signer and the person who requested the signature.
* Improve the performance and speed when assigning the **Personal Tax** service to a team member.
* When a timer is running, provide a clickable link that takes the user directly to the related task.
* Fix the issue where bulk assigning clients to another assignee or partner is slow or fails to complete successfully.
* Display helpful tooltip descriptions when hovering over the client statuses:

  * Active
  * Frozen
  * Archived
  * Draft

### New Improvements

* When bulk assigning clients, prompt the user to choose whether all associated tasks should also be reassigned.
* If task reassignment is selected, automatically transfer all tasks assigned to the previous team member to the new assignee along with the client.
* In the **Assign Team Member** modal, display:

  * Available hours for the selected team member.
  * Available capacity for the selected team.
* When viewing **Frozen Clients**, allow users to:

  * Unfreeze a single client and mark it as **Active**.
  * Bulk unfreeze multiple selected clients and mark them as **Active**.
* When viewing **Archived Clients**, allow users to:

  * Restore a single archived client to **Active**.
  * Bulk restore multiple selected archived clients to **Active**.

10/08/2026, 12:30 am - +44 7912 225796: Few small things. ![generated-image-at-00:00:00](https://loom.com/i/ebde89604cfb4d84bbe2468686e3b6bf?workflows_screenshot=true)

## Overview

Several workflows across Leads, Proposals, Digital AML, and Client Portal are not behaving as expected. Users cannot reliably delete/assign/send emails from the lead view, proposal decline actions do not update status or send the correct email, and several status indicators/log entries are missing or inconsistent. These issues make it difficult to track progress, communicate with clients, and confirm whether requests have been completed.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Open a lead in the Leeds area and review the default selections and available actions.
2. Attempt to delete, assign a task, or send an email from the lead detail view.
3. Open a proposal, add a discount, then send the proposal email.
4. Sign a proposal and then decline it.
5. Start a Digital AML request and complete the initial email/request flow.
6. Open the Client Portal e-signature flow and send documents.
7. Review the timeline/status indicators for each of the above actions.

**Observed issues:**

- Some fields are auto-selected when they should not be.
- Delete/assign/email actions are unavailable or use an outdated email popup.
- Proposal decline does not generate the correct email, update status, or add a timeline log.
- Discount values are displayed incorrectly in sent emails (e.g., 40% shown as 40 pound).
- Digital AML and e-signature statuses are not shown consistently in the UI/timeline.

## Acceptance Criteria

- Lead detail views should not auto-select incorrect values and must allow delete, task assignment, and email actions where applicable.
- Email sending should use the updated account-selection flow and send from the correct connected email account.
- Proposal decline should:
  - update the proposal status immediately,
  - generate and send the correct decline email,
  - add a decline entry to the timeline/log.
- Proposal emails should display discounts correctly (percentage vs currency) and preserve formatting such as commas in amounts.
- Digital AML requests should display request status, associated contact/director name, and timeline updates until completion.
- Client Portal e-signature requests should show top-level status, timeline entries, and correct linked contact/director relationships.
- All affected flows should update without requiring a manual refresh.

10/08/2026, 12:43 am - +44 7912 225796: @⁨AR - 14⁩ ![generated-image-at-00:00:11](https://loom.com/i/15c33d031dd2420299c9597db64b8e8e?workflows_screenshot=true)

## Overview

When an email contains multiple attached documents requiring signatures, the e-sign flow does not automatically move the user to the next required signature field/document after signing the current one. As a result, users must manually select each document and finish each one individually, and the system continues to show signed documents as unsigned/not sent in the UI and portal. This also appears to prevent or delay confirmation emails and makes it difficult to track completion status.

## Steps to Reproduce

1. Send or open an e-sign email containing multiple attached documents that each require a signature.
2. Click the first document/signature link in the email.
3. Complete the signature on the first document.
4. Observe that the flow does **not** automatically advance to the next document requiring a signature.
5. Repeat for the remaining documents.
6. Check the notifications/client e-sign/document portal views after signing all documents.
7. Observe that the documents may still appear as **unsigned** or **not sent**, and the completion state is not updated correctly.

## Acceptance Criteria

- After signing a document, the flow automatically advances to the next document/signature field that requires action.
- Multiple attached documents are treated as a single signing workflow, with clear progress through all required signatures.
- The UI clearly indicates how many signatures are required per document/page.
- Once all required signatures are completed, the system marks the documents as **signed/finished** everywhere they are displayed (email workflow, notifications, client e-sign, document portal).
- A confirmation email is sent after successful completion of all required signatures.
- Email templates reflect the correct sender/tenant branding (logo, header, footer) based on who initiated the request.
